#### `GeoClusterResult dbscanCluster(const std::vector< GeometryInfo > &points, const DbscanConfig &config=DbscanConfig{}, const GpuClusteringConfig &gpu_cfg=GpuClusteringConfig{})`
- Source: `benchmarks/geo/bench_geo_dbscan.cpp`:12
- Brief: Cluster geo points using the DBSCAN algorithm.
- Parameters:
  - `points` (const std::vector< GeometryInfo > &): Input parameter.
  - `config` (const DbscanConfig &): Input parameter.
  - `gpu_cfg` (const GpuClusteringConfig &): Input parameter.
- Return: GeoClusterResult with labels parallel to points.
- Details: Dbscan Cluster. All input geometries must be of type Point (2D WGS84). Non-Point geometries are treated as noise (label kDbscanNoise) without raising an error. Distance between points is computed with the Haversine formula on the WGS-84 sphere (same as haversineDistanceM in spatial_join.h). Complexity: O(n²) in the worst case (no spatial index acceleration). When CUDA is available and n ≤ GpuClusteringConfig::gpu_dbscan_max_n the distance computation phase is GPU-accelerated, reducing wall-clock time by up to GPU-parallelism factor. points Input point geometries. config DBSCAN parameters (epsilon_m, min_points). gpu_cfg GPU acceleration configuration (default: GPU enabled). GeoClusterResult with labels parallel to points. points Input parameter. config Input parameter. gpu_cfg Input parameter. Return value. Calls: size(), assign(), std::fill(), begin(), end(), coords(), extractLonLat(), lons().

#### `TEST(GeoVincenty, GCH24_AntipodalFallbackFinite)`
- Source: `tests/geo/test_geo_hardening_focused.cpp`:194
- Brief: n/a
- Parameters:
  - `<unnamed>` (GeoVincenty): n/a
  - `<unnamed>` (GCH24_AntipodalFallbackFinite): n/a
- Details: TestGCH-24 — geodesicDistance() returns a finite, non-negative result for nearly-antipodal points (which previously caused Vincenty non-convergence and returned -1.0; now falls back to Haversine). This test uses the CPU exact backend which contains the fixed Vincenty loop. The nearly-antipodal points are (0°N, 0°E) and (0°N, 179.9°E).

#### `GeoClusterResult dbscanCluster(const std::vector< GeometryInfo > &points, const DbscanConfig &config=DbscanConfig{}, const GpuClusteringConfig &gpu_cfg=GpuClusteringConfig{})`
- Source: `src/geo/geo_clustering.cpp`:153
- Brief: Cluster geo points using the DBSCAN algorithm.
- Parameters:
  - `points` (const std::vector< GeometryInfo > &): Input parameter.
  - `config` (const DbscanConfig &): Input parameter.
  - `gpu_cfg` (const GpuClusteringConfig &): Input parameter.
- Return: GeoClusterResult with labels parallel to points.
- Details: Dbscan Cluster. All input geometries must be of type Point (2D WGS84). Non-Point geometries are treated as noise (label kDbscanNoise) without raising an error. Distance between points is computed with the Haversine formula on the WGS-84 sphere (same as haversineDistanceM in spatial_join.h). Complexity: O(n²) in the worst case (no spatial index acceleration). When CUDA is available and n ≤ GpuClusteringConfig::gpu_dbscan_max_n the distance computation phase is GPU-accelerated, reducing wall-clock time by up to GPU-parallelism factor. points Input point geometries. config DBSCAN parameters (epsilon_m, min_points). gpu_cfg GPU acceleration configuration (default: GPU enabled). GeoClusterResult with labels parallel to points. points Input parameter. config Input parameter. gpu_cfg Input parameter. Return value. Calls: size(), assign(), std::fill(), begin(), end(), coords(), extractLonLat(), lons().

#### `double haversineDistanceM(double lon1, double lat1, double lon2, double lat2) noexcept`
- Source: `include/geo/geo_math.h`:49
- Brief: Compute the great-circle distance in metres between two WGS-84 points using the Haversine formula.
- Parameters:
  - `lon1` (double): Longitude of point 1 in decimal degrees (−180 … +180).
  - `lat1` (double): Latitude of point 1 in decimal degrees ( −90 … +90).
  - `lon2` (double): Longitude of point 2 in decimal degrees.
  - `lat2` (double): Latitude of point 2 in decimal degrees.
- Return: Distance in metres (≥ 0).
- Details: lon1 Longitude of point 1 in decimal degrees (−180 … +180). lat1 Latitude of point 1 in decimal degrees ( −90 … +90). lon2 Longitude of point 2 in decimal degrees. lat2 Latitude of point 2 in decimal degrees. Distance in metres (≥ 0). Precision: ±0.5 % for distances up to ~20 000 km. For sub-metre accuracy use Vincenty or Karney's method.

This helper compares distance results from CPU and GPU with relative tolerance. #### `GeoClusterResult kmeansCluster(const std::vector< GeometryInfo > &points, const KMeansConfig &config=KMeansConfig{}, const GpuClusteringConfig &gpu_cfg=GpuClusteringConfig{})`
- Source: `src/geo/geo_clustering.cpp`:302
- Brief: Cluster geo points using the k-means algorithm.
- Parameters:
  - `points` (const std::vector< GeometryInfo > &): Input parameter.
  - `config` (const KMeansConfig &): Input parameter.
  - `gpu_cfg` (const GpuClusteringConfig &): Input parameter.
- Return: GeoClusterResult with labels in [0, k) parallel to points.
- Throws:
  - std::invalid_argument: if k == 0 or k > number of valid points.
  - std::invalid_argument: if an error occurs.
- Details: Kmeans Cluster. All input geometries must be of type Point (2D WGS84). Non-Point geometries are skipped and receive label -1. Centroid positions are maintained in geographic (lon, lat) coordinates and distance is computed with the Haversine formula. Centroid updates use an arithmetic mean of (lon, lat) coordinates which is a valid approximation for clusters spanning less than a few hundred kilometres. When CUDA is available the assignment step (nearest centroid search) uses ECEF 3D projection and FAISS GPU FLAT_L2, providing significant speedup for large datasets (n ≥ 10000) with k ≤ 256. The centroid update step always runs on CPU. Initialisation: if config.seed == 0 the first k distinct input points are used as initial centroids. Otherwise k-means++ probabilistic seeding is performed using config.seed to seed a simple LCG PRNG. points Input point geometries. config k-means parameters (k, max_iterations, tolerance_m, seed). gpu_cfg GPU acceleration configuration (default: GPU enabled). GeoClusterResult with labels in [0, k) parallel to points. std::invalid_argument if k == 0 or k > number of valid points. points Input parameter. config Input parameter. gpu_cfg Input parameter. Return value. std::invalid_argument if an error occurs. Calls: size(), assign(), coords(), reserve(), extractLonLat(), push_back(), std::to_string(), centroids().

#### `bool pointInPolygon(double px, double py, const double *polygon, std::size_t n) noexcept`
- Source: `include/geo/geo_math.h`:139
- Brief: Test whether point (px, py) lies inside a closed polygon.
- Parameters:
  - `px` (double): X (longitude) coordinate of the test point.
  - `py` (double): Y (latitude) coordinate of the test point.
  - `polygon` (const double *): Alternating x/y pairs of polygon vertices. The last vertex is implicitly connected to the first. Must have even size ≥ 6 (i.e. at least 3 vertices).
  - `n` (std::size_t): Total number of doubles in polygon (= 2 × vertex count).
- Return: true if the point is inside (or on the boundary of) the polygon.
- Details: Uses the ray-casting (Jordan curve) algorithm. Suitable for small polygons on a projected (flat-earth) coordinate plane or for geographic polygons that do not span a hemisphere. px X (longitude) coordinate of the test point. py Y (latitude) coordinate of the test point. polygon Alternating x/y pairs of polygon vertices. The last vertex is implicitly connected to the first. Must have even size ≥ 6 (i.e. at least 3 vertices). n Total number of doubles in polygon (= 2 × vertex count). true if the point is inside (or on the boundary of) the polygon.

#### `bool validateGeometrySize(std::size_t coord_count) noexcept`
- Source: `src/geo/geo_phase2_phase3_integration.cpp`:263
- Brief: Validate geometry size does not exceed contract limits.
- Parameters:
  - `coord_count` (std::size_t): Number of coordinates in geometry
- Return: true if within limits; false if too large (error logged)
- Details: Per geo_api_contract.h §1: geometries exceeding kMaxGeometryCoordinates return GEOMETRY_TOO_LARGE. coord_count Number of coordinates in geometry true if within limits; false if too large (error logged)

#### `bool validateRingClosure(const std::vector< double > &lons, const std::vector< double > &lats, std::size_t ring_index) noexcept`
- Source: `src/geo/geo_phase2_phase3_integration.cpp`:220
- Brief: Validate polygon ring closure and produce diagnostic on failure.
- Parameters:
  - `lons` (const std::vector< double > &): Ring longitude coordinates
  - `lats` (const std::vector< double > &): Ring latitude coordinates
  - `ring_index` (std::size_t): Index of the ring (0 for exterior, >0 for interior)
- Return: true if closed; false if open (error logged)
- Details: Enforces RFC 7946 ring closure per geo_api_contract.h §1(c). lons Ring longitude coordinates lats Ring latitude coordinates ring_index Index of the ring (0 for exterior, >0 for interior) true if closed; false if open (error logged)

#### `bool validateVertexCount(const std::string &geom_type, std::size_t coord_count, std::size_t required_min) noexcept`
- Source: `src/geo/geo_phase2_phase3_integration.cpp`:241
- Brief: Validate geometry coordinate count per geo_api_contract.h §1.
- Parameters:
  - `geom_type` (const std::string &): Geometry type string (e.g., "LineString", "Polygon")
  - `coord_count` (std::size_t): Number of coordinates
  - `required_min` (std::size_t): Minimum required coordinates
- Return: true if count >= required_min; false otherwise (error logged)
- Details: geom_type Geometry type string (e.g., "LineString", "Polygon") coord_count Number of coordinates required_min Minimum required coordinates true if count >= required_min; false otherwise (error logged)

#### `bool validateWgs84Coordinate(double lon, double lat, const std::string &context) noexcept`

  - `lat` (double): Latitude in decimal degrees
  - `context` (const std::string &): Operation context for error reporting
- Return: true if valid; false if out-of-bounds (error logged)
- Details: Enforces WGS84 bounds per geo_api_contract.h §1(b). lon Longitude in decimal degrees lat Latitude in decimal degrees context Operation context for error reporting true if valid; false if out-of-bounds (error logged)
